## F34 — H1–H5 as the pre-registration actually decides them  ·  MEASURED\
\
\\\\`node tools/hypotheses.mjs\\\\`. The rules are executed as filed; where a rule is defective, it is\
executed anyway and the defect reported, because a pre-registered rule quietly improved after the fact\
is not a pre-registered rule.\
\
| | rule as filed | outcome |\
|---|---|---|\
| **H1** | supported if b1 > 0 and CI excludes 0; refuted if b1 ≤ 0 with CI excluding 0 | **REFUTED.** b1 = −0.358 [−0.488, −0.239]; within-task −0.393 [−0.541, −0.254]; sign robust to dropping any provider |\
| **H1 secondary** | penalty at ratio 5.0 exceeds that at 3.33 by > 5 pp | **MET nominally** (40.6 pp) and **not interpretable**: within-ratio-5.00 spread 79.5 pp against 85.8 pp across the whole axis |\
| **H2** | supported if exact one-sided Wilcoxon p < 0.05 **and** CI entirely below 0; refuted if CI entirely above 0; otherwise inconclusive | **INCONCLUSIVE.** Exact Wilcoxon n = 31, W+ = 261, p = 0.4046; CI [−$0.002222, +$0.001107] |\
| **H3** | CI for b1 excludes 0 (level and sidedness unspecified) | **SUPPORTED** on the stricter two-sided 95% reading. b1 = +1.05 requests [0.278, 1.777]; within-task +1.05 [0.278, 1.777] |\
| **H4** | supported if the cache coefficient's CI contains 0; refuted if it excludes 0 | **\\"REFUTED\\"** (b_cache = −0.282, CI [−0.401, −0.174]) — and the rule is unfalsifiable: a null supports the study's own negative hypothesis and a non-null is pre-declared publishable. **H4 is demoted to exploratory.** It is not evidence about cache. |\

===== f33 @ line 1904492 =====
Only **18** are unexplained. Under rule 9, **luna solves 490/508 = 96.5%**, which is an ordinary\
number and not a capability finding. The correction also inverts the comparison the sentence was\
making:\
\
| model | as published | after rule-9 exclusions |\
|---|---|---|\
| DeepSeek V4.1 Flash | 619/620 = 99.8% | **99.8%** |\
| Claude Sonnet 5.5 | 617/620 = 99.5% | **99.5%** |\
| GLM-5.3 | 308/316 = 97.5% | **99.4%** (6 credit failures removed) |\
| Qwen3.8-Flash | 608/620 = 98.1% | **98.2%** |\
| gpt-6-luna | 490/619 = **79.2%** | **96.5%** |\
| Claude Haiku 4.5 | 547/654 = 83.6% | **84.8%** |\
\
**Haiku 4.5 is the only model with a genuine solve-rate deficit, and it is the one finding the register\
never wrote down.** Its worst arm solves 53% and its best 97% — a 44-point spread on the same tasks and\
the same model. That is the quality separation batch 2 was built to produce, and it was sitting in the\
archive while the study reported a capability difference that did not exist.\
\
This is the third retraction of the same shape (F4, F14, now F28). The rule that catches it —\

## A defect in the panel that neither review could have seen\
\
The first version of \\\\`tools/cost-panel.mjs\\\\` carried **117 runs whose gateway usage was all zeros**.\
Each contributed a $0.00000 observation to its arm and pulled that arm's mean **down**, so an arm that\
was broken looked frugal. They were inflating the apparent cheapness of exactly the arms with the worst\
instrument problems. A run that consumed no tokens measured nothing, whatever its exit code, and is now\
excluded under rule 9's first clause and reported in the flow table.\

  if (arms.length < 10) problems.push(model + ': only ' + arms.length + ' arms')
  if (lo !== hi) problems.push(model + ': unbalanced, ' + lo + '..' + hi)
  if (m.noGateway) problems.push(model + ': ' + m.noGateway + ' runs without gateway usage')
  if (m.terse.rows && m.terse.real !== m.terse.rows) problems.push(model + ': terse manipulation not applied on all arms')
}

console.log('')
console.log('--- flagged ---')
console.log(problems.length ? problems.map((x) => '  * ' + x).join('\
') : '  nothing flagged')
const ji = process.argv.indexOf('--json')
if (ji >= 0) {
  const path = process.argv[ji + 1] ?? 'analysis/coverage.json'
  const { mkdirSync, writeFileSync } = await import('node:fs')
  mkdirSync(path.split(/[\\\\/]/).slice(0, -1).join('/') || '.', { recursive: true })
  writeFileSync(path, JSON.stringify({ generated: new Date().toISOString(), models: Object.fromEntries(Object.entries(models).map(([k, v]) => [k, { ...v, tasks: v.tasks.size, seeds: [...v.seeds], cells: v.cells.size }])) }, null, 1))
  console.log('wrote ' + path)
}

{"type":"assistant/message","seq":3053,"time":1790896035854,"data":{"turn":14,"step":3,"message":{"role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"reasoning","text":"**This is a very strong result — and it's a much better finding than "the batch doesn't discriminate".**

Look at the contrast:

**DeepSeek**: 5/24 discriminate; 12 too easy (100% passRate); 4 threshold.
**GLM-5.3-Flash**: **14/18 discriminate**; 0 too easy; 3 threshold.

**The same task suite discriminates 14 of 18 on GLM-Flash and only 5 of 24 on DeepSeek.**

That is *itself* a cross-model version of the paper's thesis! **Task discrimination is model-dependent.** A benchmark calibration performed on one model (which is exactly what F17 did — "check all 24 on DeepSeek before running the batch for real") would have concluded the batch was mostly useless. And note: F17's stated plan was *"check all 24 on DeepSeek (~$24) before running the batch for real"* — a calibration decision made on one model. This shows that decision was unsound: the calibration model's own capability ceiling determines whether a task looks discriminating.

Also note the mechanism: on DeepSeek 12 tasks are at **100% passRate and 7/7 solved** — DeepSeek simply solves them. On GLM-Flash the same tasks are at 86-91% passRate with 3-6/7 solved. So DeepSeek is *more capable* on these tasks, and capability compresses the spread.

Wait, careful — is that right? DeepSeek solves more, so fewer tasks discriminate. GLM-Flash fails more, so more tasks land in the discriminating middle.

Hmm, but that's exactly "a benchmark discriminates best when the model is near the task's difficulty". That's the classic ceiling/floor problem, now measured across 24 tasks and 2 models.

**And it's the same shape as the paper's thesis**: the instrument's resolution depends on the model it's pointed at, just as the cost ranking depends on the provider.

// \\\\`holm\\\\` appeared in no tool - so the stars in A2 were uncorrected. A sign-flip permutation gives\
// each contrast an exact-ish p without leaning on the bootstrap, and Holm is applied across the\
// family of seven.\
console.log('')\
console.log('  A2b  the same seven contrasts, tested and Holm-corrected (PRE-REGISTRATION §6)')\
{\
  const rndP = makeRng(0x4a17b3c9)\
  const flip = (vals, iters = 20000) => {\
    const obs = Math.abs(mean(vals))\
    let ge = 0\
    for (let i = 0; i < iters; i++) { let s = 0; for (const v of vals) s += rndP() < 0.5 ? -v : v; if (Math.abs(s / vals.length) >= obs) ge++ }\
    return (ge + 1) / (iters + 1)\
  }\
  const fam = ptcRows.map((r) => {\
    const d = pairedDiffs(r.model, 'dsh:native', 'dsh:ptc')\
    return { label: r.label, ratio: r.ratio, rel: r.relDiffPct, p: flip([...d.values()]) }\
  })\
  const sorted = [...fam].sort((a, c) => a.p - c.p)\
  const m = sorted.length\
  console.log('  ' + 'model'.padEnd(20) + 'ratio'.padStart(6) + 'diff'.padStart(9) + 'perm p'.padStart(10) + 'Holm'.padStart(9) + '  verdict')\
  for (const [i, r] of sorted.entries()) {\
    const thr = 0.05 / (m - i)\
    const passes = r.p < thr\
    r.holmThreshold = thr; r.holmPasses = passes\
    console.log('  ' + r.label.padEnd(20) + r.ratio.toFixed(2).padStart(6) + ((r.rel >= 0 ? '+' : '') + r.rel.toFixed(1) + '%').padStart(9) +\
      r.p.toFixed(4).padStart(10) + thr.toFixed(4).padStart(9) + '  ' + (passes ? 'significant' : '*** fails Holm ***'))\
  }\
  const uncorrected = fam.filter((r) => r.p < 0.05).length\
  console.log('  -> ' + uncorrected + ' of ' + m + ' survive uncorrected alpha=0.05; ' + sorted.filter((r) => r.holmPasses).length + ' survive Holm.')\
  out.sections.A2b = fam\
}\
\
out.sections.A2 = ptcRows`,\
});\

console.log('  A2b  the same seven contrasts, tested and Holm-corrected (PRE-REGISTRATION §6)')
{
  const rndP = makeRng(0x4a17b3c9)
  const flip = (vals, iters = 20000) => {
    const obs = Math.abs(mean(vals))
    let ge = 0
    for (let i = 0; i < iters; i++) { let s = 0; for (const v of vals) s += rndP() < 0.5 ? -v : v; if (Math.abs(s / vals.length) >= obs) ge++ }
    return (ge + 1) / (iters + 1)
  }
  const fam = ptcRows.map((r) => {
    const d = pairedDiffs(r.model, 'dsh:native', 'dsh:ptc')
    return { label: r.label, ratio: r.ratio, rel: r.relDiffPct, p: flip([...d.values()]) }
  })
  const sorted = [...fam].sort((a, c) => a.p - c.p)
  const m = sorted.length
  console.log('  ' + 'model'.padEnd(20) + 'ratio'.padStart(6) + 'diff'.padStart(9) + 'perm p'.padStart(10) + 'Holm'.padStart(9) + '  verdict')
  for (const [i, r] of sorted.entries()) {
    const thr = 0.05 / (m - i)
    const passes = r.p < thr
    r.holmThreshold = thr; r.holmPasses = passes
    console.log('  ' + r.label.padEnd(20) + r.ratio.toFixed(2).padStart(6) + ((r.rel >= 0 ? '+' : '') + r.rel.toFixed(1) + '%').padStart(9) +
      r.p.toFixed(4).padStart(10) + thr.toFixed(4).padStart(9) + '  ' + (passes ? 'significant' : '*** fails Holm ***'))
  }
  const uncorrected = fam.filter((r) => r.p < 0.05).length
  console.log('  -> ' + uncorrected + ' of ' + m + ' survive uncorrected alpha=0.05; ' + sorted.filter((r) => r.holmPasses).length + ' survive Holm.')
  out.sections.A2b = fam
